### Page 2

世界経済：AI・防衛関連需要を受け景況感は好調。一方でインフレ圧力の広がりも確認
 9月のグローバル製造業PMIは53.0（8月52.3）と堅調。非製造業は54.5（8月53.6）と40か月ぶりの高水準
― 製造業はAI・防衛関連投資需要を受け生産、新規受注ともに上昇。非製造業も幅広い業種で業況が改善
― 一方、仕入・販売価格指数はともに上昇。エネルギーや輸送価格上昇によるインフレ圧力の広がりを示唆
 国・地域別では米国・ユーロ圏が製造業・非製造業ともに改善。とりわけ米国の好調が目立つ
― 米国ではAI関連支出の拡大に伴い機械・設備投資および生産が増加。非製造業においても情報通信業が突出して好調
― ユーロ圏でも、AI投資に支えられたIT関連サービスが特に力強く拡大。専門・商業サービスも改善
1
製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数） 非製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数）
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数：企業に対するアン
ケート調査結果から作成した指数。新規受注や生産高、雇用、出荷遅延などの指数

に一定のウエイトを掛けて算出。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点
（出所）S&P Global、中国国家統計局より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 3

金融市場概況：投資家心理が悪化し、日米とも株安・金利低下
 AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、日米株価はハイテク株を中心に下落
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機に。原油高に伴うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に
― 投資家心理が悪化するなかで、安全資産とされる日米国債に資金が流入、前月までの金利上昇基調は一巡
 ドル円は、1ドル＝150円台後半で方向感の乏しい展開
― 日米金利とも低下し、日米金利差は概ね横ばい圏で推移
― 原油高が輸入増を通じて円安要因となる一方、日米株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化に伴うユーロ安は円高要因に
2

米国：インフレ率は高止まり、油価再加速で当面は物価高が続く懸念
 原油高や関税の影響から物価は加速
―9月のコアCPIは前月比＋0.3％（年率＋4.1％）と8月（前月比＋0.3％、年率＋3.5％）から伸びが拡大
― コアサービスでは住居費や輸送サービスなどが押し上げに寄与。コア財については、衣料品や玩具などが上昇。関税引
き上げに伴う価格転嫁が続いている模様
 足元で原油価格が再加速していることを踏まえれば、当面は物価の上振れリスクが残存する懸念
― シカゴ連銀が公表する週次の小売物価指標は9月後半から10月初めにかけて上昇しており、物価の高止まりを示唆
3
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（出所） シカゴ連銀より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：足元の消費はガソリン高や関税に伴う物価上昇で足踏みも、年末商戦は堅調な見通し
 消費は足踏み。ガソリン価格高騰や関税に伴う物価高が家計の購買力を下押し
―9月の実質小売売上高は前月比▲0.5％と減少。ガソリン小売が減少したほか、自動車や衣料品も低調に推移
 物価高は逆風も、2026年の年末商戦は堅調予想
― 米マスターカードは、実質小売売上高（除くガソリン・自動車）は前年比＋2.7％と、前年（同＋2.1％）から伸びが拡大する
と予想。雇用環境の改善などを背景に幅広い所得層で安定した消費がみられると指摘
4
（注） ガソリンはCPIを用いて実質化。その他は、8、9月のみ各品目に対応するCPIを用

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同＋8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建築・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

5
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 7

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様
6
（注）1.バブルの大きさは各産業の雇用者数
2. 対人接触度は、O*NETの職業別のPhysical Proximity（業務上、他者と物理的に近
接する度合い）を、各産業の職業別雇用者構成で加重平均した指数
3.コロナ禍からの雇用回復の影響を除くため、雇用者数の変化率は2023年末を起点
に算出
（出所） 米労働省、セントルイス連銀より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：生産は底堅く推移。AI・防衛が投資財生産をけん引、半導体増産も寄与
 2026年8月の鉱工業生産は前年比＋1.4％と底堅い結果
― 半導体関連の生産が引き続きけん引。生産能力が2022年対比1.7倍に拡大するなど生産体制の増強が背景。事実、主
要半導体メーカーによる工場新設・増強を受け、MOSロジック（GPU、CPUなど演算用）の量産が本格化
 AI・防衛分野を中心に投資財生産も堅調さを維持
― 投資関連財はAI・防衛が押し上げる形で同＋4.7％と増勢継続。国防・宇宙関連設備は同＋6.1％ と全体を上回る伸び。
地政学リスクの高まりを背景に軍用機やミサイル・防空システムなどの生産体制強化が図られている模様
7

生活費
期待より悪い 期待通り 期待より良い
（シェア）
米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

8

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
9
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 11

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
10
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（出所）Eurostat より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
13
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性

中国：政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ。人民元相場は緩やかな元高が継続
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え
 人民元相場は、中国の貿易黒字が過去最高水準を記録する中、緩やかに元高が進行し1ドル＝6.6元台に突入
― 高水準の貿易黒字を背景とした人民元買いの実需が強いことから、当面は緩やかな元高が続く見込み
― ただ、米FRBの利上げで米中金利差が拡大しており、さらなる拡大が意識されれば元安に転じる可能性も

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み
（注） コメは玄米及び精米。食品関連は、飲食料品及び農林水産物（除くコメ）

米国債券市場：インフレ加速懸念で上昇後、AI関連株の下落を受け金利は低下
 10月の米10年国債利回りは、一時5.3％台まで上昇したものの、足元では5.0％前後まで低下
― 月初は、原油高等によるインフレ加速懸念を受け、長期金利は約24年ぶりの高水準となる5.3％台まで上昇
― 中旬以降は、AI関連株の先行きに対する慎重な見方から、安全資産である米国債への需要が拡大し、長期金利は低下
 今後も、中東情勢に伴う原油価格の変動やAI関連株の動向など、金利を左右する不確実性が多く、振れの大きい展開が続
く可能性

国内債券・為替：株安を背景に長期金利は低下。ドル円は150円台後半で横ばい圏
 長期金利は、株安を背景に低下。10年金利・30年金利とも、10月上旬から30bps以上の大幅低下
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機となり、安全資産とされる日本国債に資金が流入
― 先行き、株式市場の混乱が続けば、短期的には一段の金利低下余地も。もっとも、日銀は四半期に一度のペースで利上
げを続けるとみられ、混乱一巡後の円金利は、再び緩やかな上昇基調に転じる見通し
 ドル円は150円台後半で方向感に欠ける展開。先行きも、150円台を中心とした円安水準が続く見込み
― 原油高は円安要因の一方、株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化を受けたユーロ安は円高要因。目先は両者が綱引きへ

内外株式：AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落

― 企業業績への期待から、10月初の日米株は上昇。S&P500は一時最高値を更新。日経平均も一時7万円台を回復
― その後は、米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格の急落を受け、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落。原油価格の上昇に伴

うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に。もっとも、S&P500はヘルスケア関連株等への資金シフトが進み、底堅く推移
 AIインフラ投資に対する信用不安は残存。原油価格の上昇も重石となり、日米株は軟調な推移が続くと予想
― 金利が高水準で推移する中、AIインフラの過剰投資懸念が強まり株価を下押しするリスクには留意が必要

months of the year), (2) new Kunshan capacity (total 1.2mn sheets/month or 21% of the
company’s current capacity) ramp up from December, and (3) the mass volume
shipment for several new AI projects (Vera Rubin + TPU v8 etc.) with a better demand
volume on the most high end M8.5/M9 CCL, we now expect EMC’s revenue to go up by
20% QoQ, and expect the GM will further expand to 40.9% (first time surpassing
40%).
For TUC, with an even faster product mix improvement speed (due to the lower base vs.

EMC) and the solid pricing hike uptrend for both high end and mid end CCL (15-20%
QoQ average pricing hike in 4Q), we expect the company to deliver a revenue growth
rate at 18% QoQ, and believe the GM will reach 40.8% (+3.9ppt QoQ) in 4Q26,
despite the slowing demand from key AI ASIC customer due to the transition period of
the ASIC projects whose capacity will be replaced by other high-end Ethernet switch
projects (also M8.5/M9 grade CCL).
Key update - EMC now plan to expand capacity to 15.25mn sheets/month from

5.85mn sheets/month capacity today, with solid demand
EMC’s BOD has approved additional capacity expansion plans extending into
2029/30E. EMC now targets year-end capacity of 8.70/10.45/11.95/15.25mn
sheets/month in 2027/28/29/30E (implying 27% 2026-30E CAGR), which is the first
time EMC announced its new capacity expansion plan after 1H28. EMC mentioned that
capacity expansion is supported by LTAs with customers who secure volumes (EMC does
not proactively initiate LTA negotiations). EMC indicated that despite its proactive

capacity expansion planned for 2027-30E, it would still be insufficient to meet with
customer demand secured under signed LTAs (demand under LTA extends at least 3Y
ahead).
EMC’s near-term capacity expansion remains intact, as the capacity expansion in
Kunshan (with additional 1.2mn sheets/month, 21% of current capacity) could
come online as early as Dec 2026 and continue through May 2027 (with new capacity
coming online every month), the expansion was originally scheduled in 2 phases, with

600k sheets/month each coming online in 1Q27 and 3Q27. The accelerated ramp-up
will offset the rescheduling of Guanyin capacity expansion, where the 300k sheet/month
expansions originally scheduled for 4Q26 have been deferred to 2H27, mainly due to
shortages of construction capacity in Taiwan as well as long equipment lead time +
Taiwan government’s strict policy on labor safety related requirement (however, we only
see delay in Taiwan, while all other plans are basically in-line or earlier than the

company’s original expectation).
Investment view - Solid margin beat + expanding margin, suggesting a stock
performance outlook; buy in the dip for both EMC & TUC
We notice that the BBG consensus for 2027 EMC/TUC GM is only at 38%/34%, and we
see upside risk to consensus EPS estimates for both EMC/TUC as the GM in 4Q could
both surpass 40-42%, without any possibility to see the GM to decrease in coming
quarters as the high-end CCL supply demand gap to further expand in coming years (see

here). As a result, we believe consensus 2027 GM could lift from 34-38% to 40-42%+ for
both companies in coming days, suggesting 10-25% EPS upward revision opportunity,
and recommend taking advantage to buy in this valuation dip (EMC/TUC only trades at
15x/12x of our bullish 2027 EPS estimate, which was far below the past 3 year range of

### Page 3

20-30x).  
Moreover, EMC has mentioned all of its new capacity in coming years (capacity will 
expand by 2.6x in 2030 vs. 2026; see more details above) is still not large enough to 
support customers’ LTAs volume demand, suggesting an even more favorable high-end 
CCL pricing outlook in coming years (all the way to 2030), which is in-line with what we 
discussed in our TPCA takeaway report. We expect EMC/TUC will rise the like for like 
base pricing for M7+ CCL by 10-40%, and we now expect the similar pricing hike ratio in 
2027/28, with more room to adjust if the supply shortage issue continues.  
For the new CCL generation, we exect EMC to start shipment on M10 grade CCL from 
2H27, which is only for single customers’ AI GPU projects, but still implies a CCL 
migration trend in-line with our bullish view. For M9, we only see HGX R200 UBB and 
VR200 LPU adopt this solution in 2026, but see multiple new projects in 2027 will adopt 
this high ASP project (including several 1.6T ethernet switch projects, and Trainium 4/ 
TPU v9 could also see at least 50% adoption rate of the M9 CCL; moreover, the VR300 
rack should have 50%+ of its CCL content related to M9 grade CCL). We believe this new 
technology migration trend going into 2027 will further drive both companies’ ASP and 
profit in coming quarters, suggesting an even more favorable outlook for EMC/TUC’s EPS 
growth in coming years.  
Overall, we maintain our bullish view on high-end CCL players’ business outlook and 
margin expansion trend, and expect investors to start focusing on this solid margin 
expansion trend after this clean beat on GM/OPM in 3Q26; maintain Buy on 
EMC/TUC, with a new TP of NT$11,700/NT$3,460 (from NT$10,200/NT$3,010).

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Elite Material  
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material 
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for

### Page 7

3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$11,700 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$3,460 is based on a 22x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(+2x STDV higher than the past 3-year industry average P/E multiple).

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: MediaTek (MTK) disclosed on 30 October a strong set of 
quarterly results and guidance both beating market expectations, thanks to 
a faster-than-expected ramp in its first AI accelerator project. We walked 
away from today’s call with a firmer view on its AI business - a faster ramp 
for the first project and a broadened customer base for a new project. We 
raise our forecasts and 12M TP, reiterate our Buy (1) call and suggest that 
investors take advantage of the recent stock pullbacks for bargain-hunting.

What’s the impact: Results and guidance all beat. MTK released 3Q26 
net profit of c.TWD28bn, beating our/consensus estimates by 11%/9% 
thanks to a better topline, lower opex ratio and higher non-operating gains 
(table on p.2). 3Q26 revenue finished 6%/4% above our/consensus 
estimates and topped guidance (TWD152-160bn), thanks to an earlier 
ramp of its first AI accelerators on some easing of the substrate supply 
constraint. Guidance calls for 4Q26 revenue of TWD216bn, at the midpoint 
of the range that beat our previous/consensus forecasts by 7%/3%, driven 
by the stronger ramp of the AI accelerator project which will more than 
offset muted consumer demand weakness associated with mobile phones. 
We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 1-6% and expect its AI revenue to reach 
USD16bn in 2027 and USD26bn in 2028, from USD2.7bn this year.

AI appears to be running ahead. At today’s call, management centred 
elaboration on its AI business progress. 1) It raised this year’s initial 
revenue contribution of the first AI accelerators to USD2.5bn for 4Q26, 
above its previous target of USD2bn. 2) While management keeps its AI 
accelerator (ex-HBM) addressable market unchanged at USD80bn for 
2027, it now aims for a 20% market share (previous: 15-20%), meaning 
USD16bn revenue contribution, vs. our previous forecast of USD15bn. 3) It 
expects revenue contribution in 2028 from the second AI customer (not 
from China), which supports our view depicted in our Global Tech report 
issued on 27 August, where we lifted MTK’s 2028 forecasts on the second 
customer ramp. 4) MTK aims to kick off 4Q26 with a new journey for its 
operating margin, expanding all the way into 2027 for more than 20% on 
average, echoing its previous comments that, although the AI accelerator 
business is gross-margin dilutive, it is operating-margin accretive.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we finetune our 12M 
TP for MTK to TWD5,500 (previous: TWD5,200), based on our unchanged 
4-quarter forward PER of 36x. We reiterate our Buy (1) rating with key 
downside risk of any AI accelerator ramps below our expectation.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 6-8% above the consensus 
forecasts as we believe the street has yet to factor in its better AI ramp.

30 October 2026

Firmly on track with AI ASIC transformer

➢ 3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance all beat expectations 
➢ Raise our forecasts on faster-than-expected AI ramps 
➢ Reiterate Buy (1) rating with a higher 12M TP of TWD5,500

Company profile 
Ranked No.5 in the world by 2023 fabless semiconductor revenue, MediaTek (MTK) offers diverse 
products across optical storage, digital home, smartphone, PC, ASIC, automotive and other IoT 
applications. MTK has successfully captured the industry demand cycles from optical disk drives to 
feature phones to smartphones, and now positions itself well as a platform provider to capitalise on the 
next demand cycle of the BigData/AI to help facilitate digital transformation in human lives.

### Page 5

5

MediaTek (2454 TT): 30 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 4 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
MTK’s Board of Directors (BoD) has 8 members with diverse backgrounds in foundry, IC design
and electronics. Among its BoD, 4 members are independent directors, representing 50% of total
directors. Its Chairman and CEO are not the same person, but its CEO is also one of the
directors. MTK conducts internal annual performance evaluation of the BoD, individual directors
and functional committees.
The board currently includes only one female director out of eight members, which represents
12.5% of the total. The company recognizes this gender imbalance and is actively working
toward a more inclusive and balanced board composition
Capital management 2
MTK’s dividend payout ratios in the past 5 years were above 60% with increasing cash dividend
in the past 3 years in dollar amount. In addition, MTK is committed to paying a special dividend
of TWD16/share over 2021-24 on top of its regular annual dividend. MTK is one of the leading
fabless chipmakers in the world with continued efforts in new product development for diversified
applications; therefore, it will continue to invest in its research & development. We see its
dividend payout as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1
MTK’s revenue from related parties was less than 1% of its total revenue in 2024 and transaction
terms were not abnormal compared to other customers. It also had related-party transactions
with King Yuan Electronics Co., Ltd. and King Long Tech (Suzhou) Ltd for IC testing,
experimental services, and manufacturing technology services in 2021. The purchase amount
from related parties was less than 7% of its total cost of goods sold/purchases in 2024. Thus, we
do not see meaningful impact on its business operation.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
MTK has established its "Supplier Code of Conduct", which covers areas such as labour and
human rights, health and safety, environmental protection, code of ethics, and management. It
requires all suppliers to sign a guarantee to comply with this code of conduct. MTK implements
an annual review to continuously track the actual compliance of suppliers. We believe these
measures can help MTK reduce supply chain risk.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 1
As a fabless without manufacturing plants, MTK does not produce much wastewater compared
to manufacturing companies, and thus has relatively limited impact on the environment.
MTK has initiated water conservation methods, including efficient enhancement of using water-
saving apparatuses, installing rainwater recycling tanks and using recycled water in cooling
towers.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
MTK prioritises waste reduction, classification for reuse, adherence to recycling, and reutilisation
to manage waste efficiently and maximise the benefits of recycling. Also, it chooses only
qualified partners for waste disposal and recycling, and also audits the waste processes
regularly to ensure legal disposal of its waste and to fulfil its responsibility in waste management
supervision.
E GHG emissions 1
The main source for MTK’s GHG emissions is externally purchased electricity, which accounts
for over 90% of its total emissions. To enhance energy efficiency and reduce emissions, the
company implemented several measures, including ongoing improvements to data center
efficiency, full adoption of LED lighting in offices, and optimized operation of chiller units.
MediaTek continued advancing its renewable energy strategy and alignment with global climate
initiatives such as SBT and RE100. In early 2025, the Company passed the SBTi review and set
targets based on a 2020 baseline, including a 40% reduction in Scope 1 and 2 emissions by
2030, net zero by 2050, and achieving 100% renewable energy use across global offices
(excluding data centers) by 2030.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

M
Update
Nuvoton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Losses Widen; BMC Growth and
Japan Restructuring Remain Key
3Q losses widened Q/Q amid lower gross profit, increased opex
and reduced non-operating income. Management sees scope for
faster BMC growth in 2027, with execution and Japan
restructuring critical to delivering earnings growth.
3Q losses widened: Revenue was NT$7.86bn, -1% Q/Q, +8% Y/Y, 3% above our
estimate; gross profit was NT$2.9bn, -3% Q/Q, +13% Y/Y, 6% above our forecast;

gross margin was 37.4%, -0.6ppt Q/Q, +1.7ppt Y/Y, 1.2ppt above our estimate. Loss
per share was NT$1.24, wider Q/Q, Y/Y, and vs. our expectation. Management
attributed gross margin weakness to product mix; back-end supply constraints and
substantial AI-related R&D left operational progress around 1Q behind plan.
Demand remains mixed: Auto demand has stabilized after prior Y/Y declines, but
management sees no clear rebound. PC demand was significantly weaker in 2H26

than in 1H26 with higher content per PC vs. 2025 helping cushion the impact of
lower volumes. Memory cost increases have been passed through on memory-
bundled MPUs, although some customers began pulling back in 4Q26. MCU
repricing reflects foundry and OSAT costs. Most 2027 capacity has been secured but
cost pass-through and supply availability do not establish margin expansion.
BMC and Japan remain the key milestones: Management sees scope for 2027 BMC

Y/Y growth to exceed 2026's, with existing customers and multiple new customers,
including a CSP, contributing. New customer revenue should initially be small with
fuller contributions over one to two years. Current-gen products should dominate
2027 shipments; next-gen volumes may start modestly in 2H27. Japan remains the
source of losses, making the Tower JV restructuring targeted by management for
end-March 2027 central to recovery.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Update
Greater China Technology Semiconductors  | Asia
Pacific
Cloud Semis: Growing Cloud
Capex into 2027
Hyperscalers’ spending remains strong so far, with further
expansion signaled for 2027 amid FCF turning negative. Positive
read for cloud semis (Aspeed and Montage).
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst
Daniel.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2863
Charlie Chan
Equity Analyst

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
2027 capex growth is highlighted: The Big Four hyperscalers' combined C3Q capex
was US$185bn, +90% Y/Y. CY2026 guidance stands at approximately US$170bn for
Microsoft, US$195–205bn for Google, US$135–145bn for Meta, and US$220bn for
Amazon. Google and Amazon maintained guidance; Meta raised its spending floor

US$5bn. More importantly, Microsoft expects spending to accelerate in January–
June 2027, Google anticipates a significant increase in 2027, and Amazon expects
meaningfully higher spending. Meta also plans to keep investing aggressively.
Supply constraints remain the issue: Microsoft reported C3Q26 capex of US
$45.2bn and expects C4Q spending >US$50bn, with some capacity deliveries
shifting into 1H27. Google invested US$51.4bn and turned to third-party computing

late in C3Q to address supply constraints, with usage rising in C4Q. Meta invested
US$34.9bn, including finance lease principal payments; Amazon’s cash capex
reached US$75.9bn. Inference already contributes well over half of AWS AI revenue.
Debating monetization: Microsoft generated US$18.6bn of quarterly FCF and
expects positive FCF in FY2027. Google, Meta, and Amazon reported negative
quarterly FCF of US$9.1bn, US$2.1bn, and US$33.5bn, respectively. Google also

flagged depreciation, data center operating costs, and third-party computing
expenses as near-term cloud margin headwinds, alongside TPU inventory cash
requirements. Management remains focused on attractive long-term returns.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through. Sustained server investment supports
Aspeed's and Montage's demand outlook; delayed capacity could shift revenue
between quarters, but it's just a timing issue. Approximately 75% of Microsoft’s C3Q

capex targeted short-lived assets, including CPUs, GPUs, and higher memory/storage
costs, up from two-thirds mentioned before. Google allocated ~65% to servers and
networking.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
2027 capex is highlighted: Microsoft, Google and Amazon flag higher spending;
Meta remains committed to aggressive investment.
Capacity constraints, not budget: Microsoft’s delivery delays and Google’s third-
party computing usage point to infrastructure bottlenecks.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through so far. Sustained hyperscaler spending

provides a favorable backdrop, with capacity additions raising demand.
November 2, 2026 01:07 AM GMT

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Idea
2027 price hike should be case by case amid push back from some customers:
Management said 2027 pricing negotiations could be mixed across customers even
though capacity is nearly fully loaded. Some need more capacity in 4Q26 and are
willing to pay the new post-hike wafer price. However, the company still faces push
back from some customers, likely tied to their inventories. Our recent checks
suggest  Tier 1 foundry L TA wafer pricing into 2027 could be less than 10%, below

the buyside bogey of a 25-30% blended hike. However, for some tier 2 customers,
the price hike could indeed be around 25-30%. We believe the average like-to-like
L TA wafer price could only increase by  15-20% in 2027. Stay EW.
SOI demand exceeds expectation: The company said 12-inch SOI demand already
exceeds its current expansion plan. The priority is to debottleneck and ramp existing
Missouri capacity. More than half of the newly installed tools have qualified for

volume production, and RF SOI and silicon-photonics products have entered volume
production.
4Q26 could be relatively muted: We expect 4Q26 to be relatively muted since
wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is fully loaded, and
new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin.
With this report, Lucas Wang assumes coverage of GlobalWafers.

bull camp expects. Stay EW.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Still Better Supply/Demand Dynamics into 2027-28e
3Q26 earnings and 4Q26  outlook review: 3Q26 results missed both our and Visible
Alpha (VA) consensus estimates given the July fire at the Novara, Italy facility, which
disrupted 8-inch wafer production and related SOI output. We expect 4Q26 to be
relatively muted since wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is
fully loaded and new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin. We had

expected 4Q26 gross margin to be around 25-26%, but we have now reduced that to 22-
23%.

Micron/GWC deal marks a new milestone for GWC
Micron Technology and GlobalWafers announced a deeper strategic partnership on July 9,

2026, centered on a 10-year supply agreement and US$500mn of strategic financing from
Micron to support GlobalWafers' new 12-inch silicon wafer facility in Sherman, Texas. On
its latest earnings call, GWC board proposed executing that financing as up to US$500
million of 10-year privately placed convertible bonds, with Micron as the sole subscriber,
carrying a 0.5% coupon. The agreement secures significant raw silicon wafer capacity for
Micron's long-term manufacturing plans, with the financing linked to the buildout of

GlobalWafers America's (GWA) Sherman facility.
GlobalWafers Chairwoman Doris Hsu described the agreement as the largest and longest
contract in the company's history. The two companies will also collaborate on next-
generation wafer technologies and process innovation. The Sherman plant is notable as
the only US-based source of advanced 12-inch wafers.
The agreement forms part of Micron's broader US$3bn commitment to strengthen the US
semiconductor supply chain and accompanies its target of manufacturing 40% of its

DRAM output domestically by the mid-2030s. We believe GWC is well positioned to

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
support ongoing foundry and memory capacity expansion in the US, not only for Micron
but also for TSMC and Samsung, which are already GWC's two largest customers. We
currently assume Micron/GWC L TA pricing could be US$70-80 on average for the polished
wafer (10-20% above current L TA pricing after discounts), reflecting the higher US cost
base. This would be broadly in line with, or slightly above, L TAs signed in 2021-22.
Current inventory levels remain higher than during the 2021-22 upcycle

when existing L TAs were signed
Notably, customer inventories of 12-inch wafers remain well above levels seen during the
previous industry upcycle. According to SUMCO's inventory trend data (see Exhibit 3 ),
inventory volumes and turnover days were near the lowest levels of the 2014-26 period
during 2021-22, reflecting a lean and highly utilized supply chain. By contrast, the current
cycle presents the opposite picture: shipment volumes are approaching record highs, but

customer inventories are also near record levels, with inventory days and absolute
inventory exceeding those seen even at the peak of the 2021-22 upcycle.
This backdrop differs meaningfully from the 2021-22 environment and, in our view,
supports a more moderate and delayed pricing recovery in 2027 rather than a repeat of
the broad-based increases seen during the prior cycle. In 2021-22, wafer makers held
significant pricing leverage as customer inventories sat near cycle lows despite record

shipment volumes. Fabs consumed nearly all purchased wafers with minimal inventory
buffers, creating a tight supply environment that enabled wafer manufacturers to
implement meaningful price increases across 12-inch wafers.
While shipment volumes currently remain elevated, customers also hold substantial
inventory. This provides a buffer against demand growth and moderate supply disruptions,
reducing the urgency to secure incremental wafer supply. Historically, this dynamic

weakens suppliers' pricing power. Until inventories normalize meaningfully, wafer makers
are likely to have less pricing leverage than they did entering the 2021-22 upcycle. Our
view is that 2027 L TA negotiations will focus primarily on removing current discounts
rather than driving nominal price increases. We broadly expect 2027 industry L TA pricing
increases of 15-20% in average, with Tier 2 customers' wafer price to be raised 25-30% but
Tier 1 customers would only see 5-15%.

Estimate Revisions
We cut our EPS estimates by 14%, 15% and 14% for 2026-28, respectively. The
reductions in our  forecasts primarily reflect lower wafer price hike to Tier 1 customers and
the margin pressure from overseas capacity ramp. However, we also lower our tax rate
assumption from 25% to 20% to reflect the subsidy from the US government.

Morgan Stanley Research 7
Valuation Methodology
We lower our price target from NT$900 to NT$770: The price target change mostly
reflects our earnings forecast change over  2026-28. All of of key assumptions in our DCF
model are unchanged, including  a cost of equity of 11% (beta of 1.5, risk premium of 6%
and risk-free rate of 2%), an intermediate growth rate of 13%  and a terminal growth rate
of 4%.
Our bull- and bear-case values are also lowered, from  NT$1,140.00 to NT$975.00 and NT

$450.00 to NT$385.00, respectively, implying P/Es of 32x and 13x 2027e.

32x 2027e EPS
Semiconductor industry recovers faster than
expected. Pure foundry players' utilization
rates rebound to 100%, and memory players
start to pull in more raw wafers. GWC's

2025-28 vs. 8% in 2021-23. Gross margin
recovers to over 40% vs. 37.4% in 2023.

25x 2027e EPS
Semiconductor industry recovers from a

down-cycle. Pure foundry and memory
players start to see recovery in utilization
rate, while both spot price and LTA amount
also increase gradually. GWC's total revenue
CAGR rises to >15% in 2025-28 vs. 8% in
2021-23. Gross margin stays above 25%.

13x 2027e EPS
Semiconductor industry recovers more
slowly than expected during this down-
cycle. Pure foundry players start to breach
LTAs and memory players start to cut more
raw wafer orders. GWC's total revenue

CAGR slows to 0% in 2025-28 vs. 8% in
2021-23. Gross margin declines to <20% vs.
37.4% in 2023.

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

현재주가 108,600 원

Document 295: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause , however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downsid e 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would  remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widel y 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is  
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals” . By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutini sed. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA 1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike.  But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, t he decision is justified by the need to further analy se risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that an y rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United St ates). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the H ICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1% -2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan 
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

debt-to-GDP was basically in line with Germany’s and well below Italy’s, but today it’s far
above Germany’s and closing in on Italy’s. Outside of the temporary Covid spike, Italian
debt/GDP has been stable for over a decade.

The recent underperformance is clear for equities too – and has been for over two years.
France’s CAC 40 has recently underperformed all the major European equity indices: the

Document 354: Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13028742     1

Economy 
Insurance costs hit the brakes 
16 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Property insurance (e.g., auto, homeowners, renters) payment growth has slowed considerably, according to Bank of America 
payments data. Median payments rose just 1% year-over-year (YoY) in 2026, easing further from the 6% growth seen in 2025, 
after a roughly 12% increase in 2024, suggesting that many consumers are getting some relief from rising insurance payments. 
• However, the moderation is uneven across consumers. Bank of America payments data suggests that Gen Z continues to 
experience insurance payment growth of nearly 6%, while increases for other generations are closer to 1% to 2%. Payments 
also rose nearly 1.5% for higher-income households but edged lower for those with lower incomes. 
• Lower accident rates, easing vehicle prices, and softer major-purchase growth may be reducing cost pressures, but geography 
remains a major dividing line. Florida metros recorded declines of roughly 7% to 8%, while California and several Northeastern 
metros continued to see relatively strong increases.

Insurance payments stabilize after years of painful growth 
The burden of higher property (e.g., auto, renters, homeowners) insurance payments may be easing for some. Notably, while the 
average payment increased 3% year-over-year (YoY), the median payment was only up 1%, according to Bank of America 
payments data. This suggests that most consumers have only seen a small increase in their premiums, though there may be a 
few outliers (Exhibit 1).  
The latest data stands in contrast to just two years ago in 2024, when most consumers saw their insurance payments surge 
about 12% YoY, after a nearly 8% YoY increase in 2023.   
Exhibit 1: Insurance payments have increased only modestly through August of this year, with growth peaking in 2024 
Median and average insurance payments (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Insurance payments by age show a similar pattern, with much stronger growth in 2024 across most generations followed by a 
cooling since then (Exhibit 2). Gen Z is the outlier here, with their payments still growing at a healthy rate, increasing almost 6% 
YoY for both 2026 and 2025, after a 7% increase in 2024. Meanwhile, Millennials and Baby Boomers experienced a 2% YoY 
increase so far this year, with Gen X only seeing around a 1% rise.

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Bank of America payments data by income provides even more insight. Higher-income households paid nearly 1.5% more in 
2026 than they did a year ago, while middle-income households saw only modest increases or none at all (Exhibit 3). Conversely, 
lower-income households actually paid slightly less so far this year than they did last year.  
Exhibit 3: Higher-income households are still paying more for insurance compared to last year, while lower earners are paying less 
Median insurance payments by income cuts (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Fewer accidents and lower vehicles prices may be driving insurance payments down 
But why might insurance payments be declining at all? One thing to keep in mind is that insurance companies often raise rates 
to cover events that occurred in previous years to recover costs, while adjusting for changing risks.  
For example, the crash rate – the number of vehicle crashes per one million miles driven – suggests that there was a sharp rise 
in accidents in 2021, according to the Department of Transportation (Exhibit 4). Additionally, new and used vehicle prices 
increased significantly after the pandemic, according to Consumer Price Index (CPI) data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics 
(BLS) (Exhibit 5). So, insurers were not only facing more vehicle replacements due to a higher crash rate, but a higher cost to 
replace vehicles as well.

However, the crash rate mostly declined from 2021 to 2024, and the Department of Transportation reported that the number of 
accidents likely decreased again in 2025. Also, while the cost to maintain and repair vehicles has consistently grown since 2021, 
the price to replace vehicles peaked in 2022 and has eased since then – the latter likely a more important factor in insurance 
costs than the former. Additionally, while the CPI index for motor vehicle insurance has fallen 5% YoY, it’s possible that this may 
partially be due to rebates, according to BofA Global Research. So, the price figured into CPI may not be fully realized by the 
consumer.

Another factor in the divergence between a declining CPI and rising insurance payments in our data may be the growth in the 
amount of property people are insuring now. Notably though, this growth has eased in recent years. YoY growth in both new 
home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 and has grown modestly, or even declined, since (Exhibit 6). And given that 
these major purchases are still K-shaped (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?), it could help to explain the divergence in insurance payment growth across income cohorts.

  I NSTI TUTE

Florida’s metros have seen the largest decline when compared to other major cities 
Examining different cities across the US also sheds light on insurance trends. Consumers in major California metros saw the 
largest increases in 2026, following near double digit growth in 2025, likely driven by widespread property damage from 
wildfires, according to BofA Global Research (Exhibit 7). Major coastal metros in the Northeast like Boston and New York also 
experienced sizable increases as well.  
Conversely, many cities throughout the Sun Belt like Houston, Nashville and Dallas saw flat or even declining insurance 
payments. BofA Global Research points to several reasons for these declines: part of the equation in this region is that the 
massive runup in the cost of property insurance and domestic migration to the region through 2024 has since eased or reversed 
course.   
Exhibit 7: California major metros have seen steady insurance payment growth, while Miami metros have seen declines 
Median insurance payments by core based statistical areas (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In Florida, legislation that went into effect in 2023, eventually made its way into insurance prices in 2025, leading to widespread 
declines in premiums. The state also has a “lookback period” of three years, where insurance profits can be deemed too high and 
a rebate is issued to consumers. It’s possible this rebate is factoring into declines this year, as some carriers only allow rebates 
upon renewals.  
The Sunshine State was a notable standout for declines, with Orlando, Tampa and Jacksonville each down around 8% YoY in 
2026, followed closely by Miami with a 7% decrease. Additionally, Bank of America payments data suggests that Jacksonville, 
Miami and Orlando all had median insurance payments above the national level in 2023, though their payments were fell below 
that in 2026 (Exhibit 8).  
Major metros in Texas also saw easing insurance payments relative to 2023, while Seattle, San Jose, and Riverside, CA are 
among the major metros that went from below the national median to above in the three-year period ending in 2026.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Insurance payments include renters, auto or home, but do not include insurance escrowed into mortgage payments or deducted from a 
paycheck.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 405: The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-04)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-04).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC pause to reflect
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:14 October 29 2026 Share
No change in October, though confidence in the economy's strength and two dissents point
to a hike in December.
The FOMC held at their October meeting, albeit with two dissents, Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed
President Hammack preferring to hike by 25bps at this meeting. The statement was short and, in the
press conference, Chair Warsh again declined to provide definitive guidance on the near-term policy

### Page 2

Related articles
outlook.
The Committee remains positive on economic activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding
at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Despite elevated uncertainty, “domestic
spending has been resilient”, underpinned by “strong” productivity growth and “robust” capital
investment. Chair Warsh subsequently also emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment. Labour demand and supply were represented as balanced and resilient in

both the statement and press conference.
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains
elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual
consumer inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
Along with the two dissents, this guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help the
Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter,

the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through 2027.
Towards the end of the press conference, Chair Warsh also announced a structural change in the
FOMC schedule. While there will still be 8 meetings in 2027, a meeting in the fall will focus on
monetary policy strategy versus the current stance of monetary policy. Chair Warsh then intends for 2
of the 8 meetings in 2028 to have this strategy focus. If realised, this will see the FOMC review and

vote on the current stance of monetary policy just 6 times per calendar year from 2028.

United States: Elevated inflation and high growth
02/10/2026 4
August 2026: Marked improvement in services
business confidence, from an already high level
September 2026: Manufacturing business

confidence remains stable at a high level
August 2026: Increasing inflationary pressures September 2026: Supplier deliveries do not get
longer
August 2026: Consumers are a bit more
concerned about their personal finances
August 2026: Headline inflation resists to the
upside
U.S. macroeconomic performance has remained solid since the onset of the shock, as illustrated in particular by Q2 growth
of +3.8% and +9% (annualized quarterly rate) in consumer spending and private nonresidential investment, respectively.

In the manufacturing sector, business sentiment has gained momentum despite the shock, while the impact of this shock
on the services sector has remained very limited. The immediate reaction of consumer confidence was, however, more
negative before beginning to improve, which was nevertheless interrupted in August (latest available data for households’
sentiment on their future personal financial situation). The University of Michigan’s composite indicator continued to

deteriorate in September, due to the sharp rise in fuel prices. But declining confidence still does not translate into reduced
spending—at least not at the aggregate level.
On the inflation front, the spike immediately following the outbreak of the conflict in Iran was more pronounced than in
the months following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. The decline in June still left inflation at a high level, but its stability in
July and August was notable (3.4% y/y), as was the latest—the third consecutive—slight decline in inflation excluding

energy in August (2.5% y/y, same rate as in February). Given the existing inflationary pressures, however, inflation is highly
likely to resume its upward trend in September.
Source: BLS, ISM, NFIB, University of Michigan, BNP Paribas

電子製造業 ESG 評等分配圖

瑞昱 2379 TT  ( 電子上游–IC 設計 ) 
網通規格逐步推進，待評價面更具吸引力 
投資與評價 
1. PC 表現雖較為疲弱，但受惠基建升級與高頻寬解決方案的挹注下，網通
產品需求優於預期，預估 26 年營收年增 21.8% 
2. 規格升級仍將為主要驅動力，AI 產品亦添柴火，但推估傳統應用在高基
期下成長性有限，預估 27 年營收年增 9.7%。 
3. 預估瑞昱 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 31.91/38.15 元，考量成長動能及金融
環境不確定性，以 27 年獲利預估乘上本益比區間上緣 20 倍為評價基
礎，目標價 760 元(20X FY27E EPS)，股價尚未具顯著吸引力，維持中
立投資評等。 
重點更新 
網通產品維持韌性，表現略優於預期，預估 2026 營收年增 21.8% 
網通需求受惠基礎建設持續升級與高頻寬解決方案的挹注，其中乙太網路隨
路由器、中小企業網路及伺服器採用擴大而成長，網管型交換器在企業、中
小企業及伺服器控制層應用持續提升市占 ， 與非網管型產品的差距逐漸縮小 。
Wi-Fi 方面，Wi-Fi 7 在 PC 與路由器的滲透率，預估今年約達 30%，在車用
乙太網路採用持續穩定擴大，今年營收亦呈穩定成長態勢。但在消費性電子
方面，因 DRAM 與 CPU 等零組件持續漲價推升零售價格，削弱消費性與入
門級購買力，提前拉貨效應亦趨緩，使得PC 表現研究部預估年減約一成，另
外電視出貨在年中世足賽備貨後放緩，零組件成本壓力也持續存在 。綜合考
量下，整體預估瑞昱 2026 營收年增 21.8%達 1494.6 億元。 
2027 年規格升級及 AI 為主要驅動力，但評價仍不具吸引力 
Wi-Fi 方面，Wi-Fi 7 的滲透率，預估 2027 年將達 40%，新一代 Wi-Fi 8 開
發進度順利，預計 2H27 量產，乙太網路與 10G PON 採用擴大，帶動產品
組合改善，高階網管型交換器預估也將取得更多 design win。目前伺服器產
品涵蓋管理連接與周邊功能，包括乙太網路與交換器、 SSD 控制器、 I/O 
bridge 及 USB hub 控制器，並以此為基礎進入更高速的伺服器互連，推估
2027 年營收佔比將達一成水準 。100G 光通訊解決方案正進行認證，預期
2027 年放量更明顯，400G 與 800G 解決方案開發則穩定進行，預估 2H27
推出，PC 則預估呈現持平表現。整體而言，預估 27 年營收年增 9.7%，達
1639.1 億元，以評價面來考量，目前股價仍尚未具顯著吸引力。

資料來源：台灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 2

訪談內容 
網通產品規格升級+AI 將為長期成長動能 
2026 年 Wi-Fi 7 在 PC/NB 市場滲透率達 30%，約為 2025 年的兩倍，高階商用機型導入最積
極，Wi-Fi 聯盟目標 2027 年 6 月前完成認證測試計畫，並於 2027 年 9 月正式啟動 Wi-Fi 8 認
證。正積極與客戶進行Wi-Fi 8 design-in，預期產品 2H27 開始放量，客戶產品將在正式認證後
於 2028 年更廣泛上市。Wi-Fi 8 在 Wi-Fi 7 的基礎上著重實際使用環境中的極高可靠度，以及更
低、更穩定的延遲，預估將成為廣泛採用且生命周期長的平台。

車用乙太網路在新車生產的滲透率已超過 50%，全球汽車出貨持平，成長並非依賴車輛出貨增
加，而是來自單車乙太網路埠數結構性增加、持續打入新車廠客戶，以及穩定的 design win 動
能。隨車廠電子電機架構演進，需求自然轉向更高密度的安全關鍵解決方案，產品路線圖與此趨
勢一致，正擴充更高整合度、更多埠數的車用交換器，並開發先進閘道控制器，以因應未來軟體
定義汽車的需求。

另外 AI ASIC 機會目前處於與客戶接觸的初期階段 ， 基礎為高速連接與介面IP 的優勢 。 邊緣AI(含
地端伺服器與邊緣伺服器)是推動規格升級、提升單機價值的結構性機會，而非僅依賴PC 需求復
甦的成長動能。邊緣 AI 對更高頻寬、更低延遲、更佳功耗效率與更豐富多模態互動的需求，可
為產品組合創造更高價值的解決方案。

供應端部分預期 2027 年改善將較明顯，工程端也導入 design for availability 原則，在可行範
圍內降低對受限物料的依賴。成本漲幅已超過可合理吸收的程度，與客戶逐案討論價格調整。

### Page 3

瑞昱 ESG 政策 
瑞昱主要從事 IC 設計，產業類別歸類在半導體行業中，依據專門為投資人設計的永續揭露框架 SASB
準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水管理、廢棄物和危險材料
管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材料採購與效率。由於瑞昱
為 IC 設計公司，研究部將篇幅著重在產品設計面，並因員工對於IC 設計公司而言是相當重要的資產，
故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述，其餘溫室氣體排放及產品設計與生命週期管理等則於後簡述。

1. 員工健康與安全： 
瑞昱重視及關心同仁的身體健康與生活狀態，以優於法令之規定，每年定期實施免費健檢服務。對於健
康檢查結果為重大 ( 含輕度 ) 異常同仁，依異常項目、病情及個人健康情形，於複檢前、後分別安排
廠醫諮詢及衛教說明，持續追蹤健檢結果。與新竹臺大分院新竹醫院合作之健康諮詢服務，每月安排 9 
場次駐廠醫師健康諮詢服務，每場次由專業醫師提供  3 小時諮詢服務，2022 年度共計服務 384 人
次。

圖 1 歷年健檢人數統計

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 員工多元化和包容性： 
2022 年瑞昱全集團全球員工人數為 6,828 人，海外員工註人數佔總 員工人數約 36.9%，其中 72% 
以上同仁擁有碩士、博士學歷。瑞昱半導體以臺灣為主要營運據點， 臺灣地區員工人數為 4,943 人，
其中正式員工人數 4,826 人，約聘員工人數 117 人，男性員工所佔 比例約 80.46%、女性員工所佔
比例約 19.54%，其中 77% 以上同仁擁有碩士、博士學歷。在 2022 年所聘僱的人力中，無論在正
式員工、男性員工、女性員工等類別指標，皆有明顯成長。 
圖 2 近三年員工人數

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 4

3. 溫室氣體排放： 
瑞昱在 2022 年溫室氣體排放共計 26,462.8729 tCO2e ，其中直接排放(範疇 1)為 808.4856 tCO2e，
分別為固定排放源(緊急發電機之柴油)、移動排放源(公務車之汽油)及逸散排放源(冷媒、化糞池、滅火
器)，瑞昱因無生產製程，故無製程排放源；間接排放(範疇 2)為 25654.3873 tCO2e，主要來源為外購
電力，占整體排放量 96.9%。

圖 3 溫室氣體排放量、強度

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

4. 水和廢水管理： 
瑞昱於 2022 年持續成功開發出多項新一代產品，在各種應用功耗 （瓦數） 表現上比前一代產品功耗減
少、面積也縮小，在長時間及大量傳輸時更具顯著降低耗能之效果，充份體現節能與對環境之友善，並
帶給終端用戶更佳的使用體驗。

圖 4 產品設計成效

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

Document 508: インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く ～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実_a6041851

Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 21 日

1/3 
インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く 
～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実現へ難しい対応が続くと予想される～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ インドネシア中銀は 10 月 20～21 日の理事会で、政策金利のＢＩレートを４会合連続で
5.75％に据え置いた。MSCI が市場の透明性やガバナンスへの懸念を背景に 「スタンドアロー
ン市場」への格下げ案を示し、株安・ルピア安が進んだことで、一部では利上げ観測が浮上
していた。しかし、中銀は５～６月の累計100bp の利上げで物価安定には十分との認識を示
し、預金ファシリティ金利を 25bp 引き上げて 5.00％とするとともに、為替ヘッジ取引への
優遇措置を強化し、通貨安定と海外資金の流入促進を図る判断を示した。 
○ 一方で、 景気下支えや雇用創出に向けて、 預金準備率を11～12 月に段階的に引き下げ、 銀行
の貸出余力を拡大する方針を示した。原油高に伴うインフレ圧力や、米国の金融引き締めに
よるドル高圧力を警戒する一方、国内消費の底堅さや民間投資の持ち直し、外需の回復など
を踏まえ、2026 年の経済成長率見通しは「＋4.9～5.7％」との従来の見通しを据え置いた。 
○ 中銀を巡ってはここ数年、政策目標に物価・通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用創出を支援
する役割が追加されてきたほか、政治介入に対する懸念もある。こうしたなか、先行きも政
府の景気下支え要請と金融市場の通貨安定要求との「板挟み」となり、様々な政策目標の実
現を目指す観点から、一段と難しい政策運営を迫られる状況が続くと予想される。 
【インドネシア中銀、市場の混乱で一部に政策金利の引き上げ観測も、４会合連続で据え置き】 
インドネシア銀行（中銀）は、10 月 20～21 日に開催した月例理事会において、政策金利である
ＢＩレートを４会合連続で 5.75％に据え置くことを決定した。一方で、預金ファシリティ金利を
25bp 引き上げ、5.00％とした。中銀は、５月の月例理事会でルピア相場の安定を目的に50bp の大
幅利上げを決定したものの、その後も金融市場ではルピア安の動きが収まらず、株式、債券も下落
するトリプル安に直面した。このため、中銀は６月９日に開催した週次理事会で 25bp の緊急利上
げを実施するとともに、ルピア相場の安定に向けた追加対策を発表した。さらに、６月の月例理事
会でも 25bp の追加利上げを実施し、約１ヵ月の間に累計 100bp もの利上げを行うなど、通貨安定
に向けて金融引き締めを強化した。その後は、ルピア相場が落ち着きを取り戻したことから、前回
の９月会合まで３会合連続で政策金利を据え置き、様子見姿勢を維持してきた。 
しかし、 足元では同国の金融市場が再び混乱に直面している。 きっかけは、 指数算出会社のMSCI

### Page 2

2/3 
が 10 月15 日、同国市場を「スタンドアローン市場」に格下げする案を示し、市場参加者との協議
を開始したことにある（注１）。MSCI は
１月、同国株式市場の情報開示に懸念を
表明し、透明性向上への取り組みが進ま
なければ、フロンティア市場に格下げす
る可能性を示唆した。これを受けて、当
局は最低浮動株比率の大幅引き上げな
ど、海外投資家の懸念解消に向けた対策
を打ち出し、市場改革を加速させる姿勢
を示した。しかし、その後も市場参加者
からは、協調的な取引行為への疑念が解
消されておらず、実効性のある法執行も
確認できないとの指摘が MSCI に寄せられた。また、フロンティア市場への格下げでは、問題の深
刻さが適切に反映されないとの意見も寄せられた。このため、MSCI は 2027 年４月末まで市場参加
者の意見を募り、 同年６月末までに最終判断を示すとした。 協議を通じて市場のガバナンスや機能
の実質的な改善を裏付ける明確な証拠が確認されない限り、 格下げを進める方針である。 この発表
を受けて、主要株価指数（ジャカルタ総合指数）は一段と下振れしており、ルピア相場も下落して
いる（図１） 。 
こうしたなか、金融市場においては、月例理事会を前に、中銀が政策金利の引き上げを含む市場
安定策を打ち出すとの見方が浮上した。また、中銀は今回、会合後の記者会見の開始時刻を前倒し
すると発表した。中銀では、７月にペリー前総裁が任期を２年弱残して突然辞任し、上級副総裁で
あったデストリ・ダマヤンティ氏が総裁代行を務めた後、９月２日に総裁へ正式就任した。今回の
月例理事会は、デストリ氏の正式就任後２回目の定例会合に当たる。しかし、中銀は今回もＢＩレ
ートの引き上げを見送る一方、 預金ファシリティ金利の引き上げや為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置の
強化を通じて、市場の安定を図る判断を示した。

【中銀は様々な政策目標の実現に向け、政府と市場の間で「板挟み」となることが予想される】 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 今回の決定について 「イラン情勢の緊迫化や米国の金融引き締め
による対外的な圧力が続くなかでのルピア相場の安定」、「2026 年と 2027 年の物価目標の達成」を
目的とする政策ミックスの一環との考えを示した。一方で、 景気下支えと国内の流動性確保に向け
て、 預金準備率の引き下げを決定した。 一般商業銀行向けは現行の９％から、11 月１日に８％、12
月１日に７％へ、 イスラム銀行向けは現行の7.5％から、11 月１日に6.5％、12 月１日に5.5％へ、
それぞれ段階的に引き下げる。さらに、為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置を強化し、海外からの資金流

注１ 10 月16 日付レポート「インドネシア株に逆風、MSCI が「スタンドアローン」への格下げ検討」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666610.html）

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
入を促す方針を示した。 
世界経済については「イラン情勢の悪化による原油価格の高止まりを受けて、 景気の下押しとイ
ンフレ圧力が併存している」との認識を示す一方、 「2026 年の世界経済成長率は＋３％となる」と
の従来見通しを維持した。 また、 「米国で追加利上げの可能性が高まっているうえ、 財政政策を理由
に米国債金利は高止まりしている」 として、 「新興国への資金流入が抑制され、 ドル高圧力が続く可
能性がある」との見通しを示した。 
一方、 同国経済について 「財政支援や消費者心理の改善を追い風に家計消費は底堅く推移してい
る」としつつ、足元では「企業マインドの改善を背景に民間投資に持ち直しの兆しがでている」と
の認識を示した。先行きは「外需の増加
も見込まれる」として、 「2026 年の経済
成長率は＋4.9～5.7％になる」との従来
見通しを据え置いた。そして、対外収支
も「世界的な不確実性の高まりの影響を
緩和すべく、国際収支を強化する必要が
ある」 としつつ、 「９月末時点の外貨準備
高は輸入の5.3 ヵ月分を有する」とし、
「2026 年の経常赤字はＧＤＰ比▲2.4～
▲1.6％」になると３会合ぶりに見通し
を示した。ルピア相場については「為替
介入やヘッジ取引への優遇措置などを通じた安定強化を図っている」 と従来からの対策を強化する
考えを示した。 そして、 物価動向については 「政府が設定した目標の範囲内で維持されている」 （図
２） ほか、 足元では 「天候不順による食料インフレの懸念が高まっている」ものの、「目標域内に収
めるべく、為替相場の安定に加え、政府も食料インフレ対策を強化する」との見方を示した。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだ中銀のデストリ総裁は、MSCI による発表について 「株式市場の動向を
注視している」との見方を示した。そのうえで、政策運営について「的を絞ったものにしたい」と
して、 「海外からの投資の呼び込みに資するものにしたい」との考えを示した。そして、 「我々は５
月以降すでに100bp の利上げを実施しており、 物価安定には十分」 、 「海外投資家に対してインセン
ティブを提供している」との考えを示した。先行きの政策運営については「データ次第になる」と
して明言を避けた。また、ソリキン副総裁は、預金準備率引き下げの狙いについて「雇用創出を目
的としたもの」 、 「銀行の貸し出し余力の拡大を目指したもの」と説明した。そして、今回の政策対
応について「様々な政策目的に対応することを目指したもの」と説明した。 
こうした発言の背景には、ここ数年、中銀に対して、物価と通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用
創出を支援する役割も求められるようになっていることがあると考えられる。 政治介入が懸念され
る動きもみられるなか、中銀の政策運営はこれまで以上に難しくなっている。先行きも、中銀は政
府からの景気下支え要請と、金融市場からの通貨安定要求との「板挟み」に置かれるとともに、難
しい政策運営を迫られる局面が続くと予想される。                  以 上

Document 519: スペインは解散総選挙へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/スペインは解散総選挙へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-07

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日

MURC Focus

スペインは解散総選挙へ

～右派連立政権が成立する見込み～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 解散総選挙に臨んだサンチェス首相 
スペインのペドロ・サンチェス首相が 10 月 5 日、議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に総選挙（定数 350、比例代表制）
を行うと発表した。住宅価格や家賃の高騰に対する政府の緊急政令案が、10 月 2 日に下院で否決されたことを
受けて、少数与党による政権運営の行き詰まりを意識した首相が、国民に信を問う決断をした形である 。ただし、
サンチェス首相が率いる中道左派の与党・社会労働党（PSOE）の支持率は低迷している（図表 1）。

もともと PSOE の人気は低迷しており、前回 2023 年 7 月の総選挙でも、ライバル政党である中道右派の国民
党（PP）が第一党となっていた。しかしサンチェス首相は他の左派政党や地域政党の協力を受けて組閣し、政権
を維持した経緯がある。しかし首相周辺の汚職疑惑などから PSOE の人気は回復せず、次期の総選挙では PP
と同根の右派政党である Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。
